# Whole-repository read, the cockpit pass (uncapped) — 2026-10-02

**What this is.** The third part of the read the Owner asked for: `apps/desktop` — the Rust crates, the frontend and the cockpit's own documents — re-read with no cap on findings. It follows [`WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-01.md`](WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-01.md) (capped, 932 findings) and [`WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-02.md`](WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-02.md) (the engine, tools and documents, uncapped). It is a list of findings, not of fixes: **every item below is OPEN.**

> **Found while closing these, 2026-10-08 — not one of the 1370.** `Memory.writeRecord.test.tsx` failed
> on CI run 37821123738 and passed here. Three of its four panel lookups were a bare
> `document.querySelector` straight after `select`, racing the row's own record-state read; the fourth
> had been fixed for exactly this after run 35468281628. Reproduced on demand by delaying that read
> 400 ms: the old file fails those three, the new one passes all ten. All four go through one waiting
> helper now.

> **Found by CI on 2026-10-12 — not one of the 1370.** `Automations.governed.test.tsx` failed once on
> run 38307999515, on a pull request that touched no frontend file, with the page announcing
> "Evening digest" and showing "Morning digest" selected. It is a defect in the page, not in the
> test: the effect that selects the first row when nothing is selected held `selectedId === null`
> from the render that scheduled it and assigned the first row outright, so a click that landed
> before it ran was overwritten. Not reproduced by repetition (12 runs here, six with every core
> busy); reproduced every time by delaying that effect 300 ms, and gone with the same delay once
> the effect fills an empty selection through a functional update. Why the effect ran late on that
> runner was not established.

## How it was run

- **35 slices** of at most 300 KB cut from the 527 text files under `apps/desktop` (9.1 MB; images, fonts, lockfiles and generated schemas excluded), one read-only reader per slice, plus **four sweeps**: duplicated Rust, documents against code, dead code and placeholders, tests that do not test.
- Every finding went to a second agent, 25 at a time, told to refute it by opening the cited line: **1780 confirmed, 82 refuted, none left unverified**. Of the confirmed, 410 were already in one of the two earlier records and are left out. **1370 remain** and are listed below.
- **The readers did not finish every file.** They named 168 files they opened and did not read end to end — most of the large Rust sources among them. The list is §4. A finding count from a file on that list is a floor, not a total, and "nothing was reported" about such a file means nothing.
- No reader ran `cargo`, `npm` or a CI kit. "Confirmed" means a second agent agreed after opening the line: ◑, not ✅. **The Builder reproduced none of these.**
- The working tree moved under the readers: five pull requests merged while they ran (`#325`–`#329`). None changed a source file under `apps/desktop`; one changed comments in `provision/src/audit_signer.rs`.
- The full text of each finding — evidence, the verifier's note, the other location of a duplicate — is in [`archive/whole_repo_read_3_2026-10-02.json`](archive/whole_repo_read_3_2026-10-02.json), keyed by the `R3-` id used here.

## The numbers

- By severity: 2 high · 80 medium · 1288 low.
- By kind: 324 stale-claim · 236 bug · 218 duplicate · 211 dead-code · 177 test-defect · 154 design-contradiction · 42 security · 8 other.

## 1. High

### R3-0001 · `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_turn_ipc.rs:229` — A committed reply longer than 8192 bytes cannot be delivered: the reply frame uses the request cap

- Evidence: CommittedMessage.body is 'the exact ... accepted-output bytes' (output cap MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES = 8_388_608), but broker/src/main.rs:708-710 does `encode_frame(&body)` which refuses > MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD_BYTES = 8192, and src/governed_turn.rs:24 caps the read at 8192+64. The turn commits, then the connection fails.
- Second agent: Read end to end: ladder_executor.rs:403 pulls output up to MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES (8_388_608), verify_and_accept commits it, then broker/src/main.rs:708-710 serializes the whole result and calls encode_frame, which refuses > MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD_BYTES=8192 (ipc_framing.rs:58); the client also caps at 8192+64 (src/governed_turn.rs:24). No body bound exists in broker_orchestrator/governed_verification, and the default max_output_tokens is 4096, so a committed reply over ~8 KB fails the connection after co…
- Suggested fix: Give the broker->renderer reply its own bound sized for a full committed body (or chunk it), on both the broker write and the app read, and add a test with a body over 8 KiB.

### R3-0002 · `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2725` — Scheduler panics on a trigger ending in a multibyte character and poisons the DB mutex (found checking the TS 'port')
- Status: **CLOSED before this pass, read in the code 2026-10-13** — by `K-03`: `parse_interval_ms` takes the unit with `strip_suffix`; no `split_at` is left in it.

- Evidence: `rest.split_at(rest.len() - 1)` is a BYTE index: `every: 5м` gives a 3-byte `5м`, index 2 is not a char boundary, so it panics inside run_due while lib.rs:460 holds `state.db.lock()`. commands.rs:27 `locked` refuses a poisoned mutex; create validates length only. Not run, read only.
- Second agent: repo.rs:2725 is `rest.split_at(rest.len() - 1)` on a byte length; run_due_halves (:2782) calls it for every ENABLED automation while lib.rs:460 holds `state.db.lock()`, create_automation checks length only, and Cargo.toml sets no panic=abort. A scratch rustc copy of the function (not the app, no cargo) panicked on `every: 5м` with 'end byte index 2 is not a char boundary' and left a held std Mutex poisoned; commands.rs `locked` refuses a poisoned mutex. Precondition: the automation must be arme…
- Suggested fix: Split on the last char (`rest.chars().last()` / `char_indices`) and add a non-ASCII trigger case to the core tests.

The Windows arm of the Rust change is not compiled on any runner.
- **R3-0014** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_turn.rs:24` — A committed governed reply larger than ~8 KB cannot be returned: 8192-byte reply frame vs 8 MiB output *Evidence:* MAX_REPLY_BYTES = MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD_BYTES(8192)+64. The reply carries `message.body` (governed_turn_ipc.rs:235); output may be 8_388_608 bytes (governed_output_pull.rs:149). broker/src/main.rs:709 `encode_frame(&body)` fails AFTER run_governed_turn committed, so the renderer gets broker_transport_f… *Fix:* Size the renderer reply frame for the maximum body (or chunk it) and make an over-size reply a blocked verdict decided before commit. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — this is `K-01` of the eleventh audit (P1: a verified output can be committed and then become undeliverable), and it needs a design review, not a patch: the reply frame and the output limit are two contracts. Recommendation: a chunked pull of the committed body, so the reply frame carries a handle and never the text.

Release and packaging are his. Recommendation: build only the `.deb` on Linux until the other bundles carry an anchor installer; a package that always refuses on first launch is worse than no package.

- **R3-0020** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:162` — fmtTime parses the backend's epoch-ms text with new Date(), so no message time is ever shown *Evidence:* `const d = new Date(iso); if (Number.isNaN(d.getTime())) return '';` — brops_core::now() returns ms-since-epoch as TEXT (core/src/lib.rs:62) and timestamps.ts states `new Date("1700000000000")` is an Invalid Date. ### security (9)

- **R3-0056** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/broker/src/main.rs:342` — Shipped broker builds the §2.5 floor's principal set with the three shapes the proof driver's copy names as the defect; ALREADY drifted *Evidence:* main.rs:345 '.map(|m| m.values().filter_map(|v| v.as_u64().map(|u| u as u32)).collect()).unwrap_or_default()'. tcb_verify.rs:73-78: filter_map, unwrap_or_default and 'as u32' each 'REMOVES a principal from the set ... a floor asked about nobody passes'. *Fix:* Move the strict principals() parser into brops_broker and have main.rs refuse a uids block that is not stated whole. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the broker stays fail-closed on a `uids` block that is missing, empty, or holds a value that is not a uid; held by a source-order test, not by starting a broker. REPRODUCED in the preflight: a string uid gave a MET floor row.
- **R3-0057** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/agent_bundle.rs:250` — Capability enforcement is self-declared: covers() checks what a step says it requires, and the runner writes without checking any capability *Evidence:* L124-126 '`capabilities` ... enforced: a step may not require a capability the grant withholds'. covers() L251-256 only compares step.requires to the grant. repo.rs:3201-3228 Store arm calls knowledge::create with no WRITE_LOCAL check; grep WRITE_LOCAL in repo.rs hits only test fixtures. *Fix:* Derive the required capability from the step kind (Store => WRITE_LOCAL) at run time and refuse when the grant lacks it, instead of trusting step.requires. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a `store` step declaring nothing was covered by a grant holding only `READ_LOCAL`. `Grant::covers` now holds the grant to what the step's kind uses as well. It is enforced there and not in the runner's arm: the runner calls `verify` before every run, so a second copy would sit behind this refusal. Every grant the build writes carries `WRITE_LOCAL`, so no shipped bundle changes.
- **R3-0058** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/key_manifest.rs:414` — check_and_persist compares against the caller's in-memory floor and rewrites the file every call, so a stale process lowers a floor another process raised *Evidence:* L419-420: `check_and_advance(floor, manifest)` then unconditional `write_floor_atomically(path, ...)`. broker/src/manifest_resolver.rs:197,384 holds the floor in a Mutex loaded once. Same epoch+hash returns Ok, so an old process rewrites {5,h} over an on-disk 7. *Fix:* Re-read the on-disk floor under an exclusive lock inside check_and_persist and refuse/skip the write when disk is already at or above the value being written. **CLOSED before this pass, by `L-39`** — the advance is compared with the floor on disk under a lock; read in the code 2026-10-13.
- **R3-0059** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/anchor.rs:581` — The custody probes use fixed file names and read a planted unwritable file as 'directory not writable' *Evidence:* L581 'let probe = dir.join(PROBE_NAME); match std::fs::File::create(&probe)' and L870 '.brops-custody-probe-parent'; PermissionDenied => refusal recorded. Reproduced: a mode-000 file of that name in a directory I can write makes O_CREAT|O_TRUNC|O_WRONLY return EACCES. *Fix:* Probe with create_new on an unpredictable name (as posix_euid already does) and treat AlreadyExists as unmeasurable. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with two planted read-only files a writable directory passed its own write probe and every entry's delete probe; only `/tmp` above it refused. Both probes are one `create_new` on a random name now. The sweep also found the parent probe's unmeasurable arm held by no test; it has one. Not run on Windows.
- **R3-0060** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS omits engine/config, where the trusted-key registry lives *Evidence:* The list has engine/runtime, engine/schemas, engine/contracts, engine/laws only. engine/config/trusted-keys.json and protected-control-plane.json exist and are unprotected; this file's own test (line 4827) names `engine/config/trusted-keys.json` as the registry that would answer. *Fix:* Add engine/config to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS and to the required list in the_trust_surface_covers_the_things_that_decide_what_verified_means. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `engine/config` is on the list and in the test's required set; removing it turns that test red.
- **R3-0061** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS omits engine/tools, which holds the receipt signer and supervisor *Evidence:* engine/tools/ has brops_receipt_signer.py ("the ONLY component that holds the receipt-signing private key"), brops_signer_service.py, brops_supervisor_service.py, brops_supervisor_attest.py, broctl.py. Only the repo-root `tools` entry is listed; engine/tools is writable by a model turn and never sn… *Fix:* Add engine/tools to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `engine/tools` likewise.
- **R3-0062** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — The guard's own source file is not on the protected list *Evidence:* BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS lists src/engine_trust.rs, governance.rs, governed_turn.rs, governed_selftest.rs but not apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs, which defines the list, TrustSurfaceGuard and tool_args. - **R3-0064** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:3925` — A test pins commands.rs as writable although it holds the first production-gate refusal *Evidence:* `for open in ["apps/desktop/src", "apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs"] { assert!(!BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS.contains(&open) ...` — commands.rs:1408 defines governed_verification_unconfigured() and GOVERNED_TRUSTED_MANIFEST_PROVISIONED, refusal 1 in CLAUDE.md §6. *Fix:* Move the provisioning probe into a protected module (or protect commands.rs) and drop the assertion that it must stay writable. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — the test's own comment says why `commands.rs` stays writable ("a boundary that covers everything is a revocation"); moving the provisioning probe out of it is a design change.

- **R3-0083** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/audit-signer/src/custody.rs:144` — The anchor key is written non-atomically, so the race the code claims to handle (and a crash mid-mint) ends in a permanent Corrupt refusal *Fix:* Write the seed to a temporary file and publish it with an exclusive link/rename so a reader never sees a partial key. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED as a race: eight starts in one directory, 3000 rounds; on the old code a start read an empty `anchor.key` and was refused (round 643 here). The seed is written whole under a staging name and published with `hard_link`, which refuses an existing name; what is accepted as a key did not change. NOT compiled or run on Windows from here, the only place the service runs; a filesystem without hard links now fails the mint.

This crate's steps are spliced in before the start; the app's unelevated step stays last. Read, not assumed: `apply()` writes the allowlist before it registers the service at all. Renaming the field changes the supervisor's frame; making the child a group leader changes how the privileged recorder chain starts, and that path runs only under root provisioning. Recommended: `process_group(0)` on the spawn, proven in the live kit.

SQLite cannot alter a default, so the fix is a table rebuild in a new migration. Recommended: with `R3-0312`.
- **R3-0102** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/agent_bundle.rs:174` — GRANT_SCHEMA is 2 and 'a v1 and a v2 grant are different objects', but verify() never checks grant.schema, flow.schema or any artifact_type *Fix:* Refuse in verify() unless grant.schema == GRANT_SCHEMA, flow.schema == 1 and each artifact_type equals its constant. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED through whole re-sealed bundles: a grant stating `schema: 1`, a flow stating `schema: 2`, a manifest stating `schema: 2`, and each of the three carrying another file's `artifact_type`, all loaded. Each is held to the number and the type this build writes, which are constants now instead of literals at the writing sites. No new refusal name: a file of another schema is that file's `…_unparseable`. Six mutations, six reds. **A behaviour change:** a bundle built by a future schema is refused by this build rather than read as if it were this one.
- **R3-0103** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/agent_bundle.rs:534` — max_steps is a bound the flow file sets on itself; no runtime ceiling exists *Fix:* Compare against a compiled constant (the 8 that build() writes) rather than the file's own field. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: nine steps under `max_steps: 100` verified. Both directions mutated, both red.

- **R3-0105** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/bin/ladder_output_pull.rs:519` — served_chunk_sha256 is the digest of the base64 text, not of the bytes it is documented as *Fix:* Decode the base64url before hashing, or rename the field served_chunk_b64_sha256. **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (`L-32`)** — the digest and a new `served_chunk_len` are of the decoded bytes; text that does not decode has neither.
- **R3-0106** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/bin/produce_agent_artifact.rs:171` — An unparseable `touched` column is exported as null instead of failing the evidence export *Fix:* Propagate the parse error with `?`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with one `flow_receipts` row whose `touched` is not JSON, the export succeeded and wrote `null` for it. The export is a function now (moved unchanged first, so the test could see the old behaviour red), and an unreadable list fails it, naming the column. The real producer was then run where CI runs it, into a fresh directory, and `check_produced_artifact.py` is GREEN on its output. **Seen on the way, not fixed:** run a second time into a directory that already holds an artifact, the producer fails with `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` — with or without this change. CI always starts from an empty directory.
- **R3-0107** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/broker_orchestrator.rs:138` — settle() failures are discarded, so a committed message can leave its broker_turns row 'live' and later be recorded as 'blocked' *Fix:* Surface a failed settle (log/audit it and reconcile from the committed message) instead of `let _`, and never let expiry overwrite a turn whose messa… **CLOSED before this pass, read in the code 2026-10-13** — by `L-33`: the committed settle runs inside the persist transaction (`persist_committed_with`); the two `let _` left are the Blocked settles.
- **R3-0108** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/broker_turns.rs:412` — An empty receipt_id or nonce is reported as a replay *Fix:* Reject empty ids before the INSERT with LedgerRefusal::Fault (or a dedicated variant) and assert the exact refusal in the test. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: on a fresh ledger an empty `receipt_id` was `ReceiptReplay`. An empty id or nonce is `Fault`, decided before either table is touched, and writes nothing.
- **R3-0109** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/credentials.rs:138` — unbind records a 'credential.unbound' audit event even when no binding existed *Fix:* Record the event only when the DELETE affected a row, or record a distinct no-op event. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: unbinding a slot nobody had bound left a `credential.unbound` evidence row. The row is written only when a binding was removed; the call is still `Ok`.
- **R3-0110** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/db.rs:33` — A database migrated by a newer build opens without refusal; SCHEMA_VERSION and current_version are read only by tests *Fix:* In open(), refuse when current_version(conn) > SCHEMA_VERSION. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with one row in `_migrations` above `SCHEMA_VERSION`, `migrate` returned Ok and the database opened. It is refused before anything runs against it, naming the version found and the one this build knows. **A behaviour change:** an older build no longer opens a database a newer build migrated — it stops at start-up and says why.
- **R3-0111** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_message_store.rs:48` — A broker database created under the old CHECK refuses every demonstration_custody commit, with no migration *Fix:* Detect the old constraint in create_schema and rebuild the table, or refuse at startup with a message naming it. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — already closed by #407 (`b6f70b7`, audit `L-35`): `create_schema` recognises the former constraint in `sqlite_master` and rebuilds the table in one transaction, and refuses a shape it does not know. MEASURED today: with the migration call replaced by `Ok(())`, three named tests go red.
- **R3-0112** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_message_store.rs:94` — The membership half of verify_readback cannot fail from its only caller and no test reaches it *Fix:* Add a test calling verify_readback("unverified", ..., "unverified") or drop the membership clause and its 'Both halves matter' comment. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED by deleting the membership clause: all 22 tests of the module stayed green. Every case that used a label outside the set also made the row and the caller disagree, so the equality answered first. The new test has them AGREE on four labels outside the set, and on each label inside it as the control; with the clause deleted it goes red.
- **R3-0113** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_output_pull.rs:396` — The refused arm of parse_read_reply does not check the other four keys the frame must carry *Fix:* On the refused arm require output_stream_id, seq, bytes_b64 and eof to be present, with bytes_b64 and eof null. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a reply saying `ok: false` while carrying bytes was read as a clean refusal. The refused arm requires the fixed frame: `bytes_b64` and `eof` null, the id a string or null, the seq a non-negative integer or null. The engine's and the bridge's builders produce exactly that; the live ladder's refusal cases are where the real frames meet it.
- **R3-0114** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_prepare.rs:162` — PrepareError's machine name and field are discarded by the only production caller, so a bad BROPS_GOVERNED_* override is undiagnosable *Fix:* Log the PrepareError (Display) on the broker's stderr before mapping it to UpstreamBlocked.
- **R3-0115** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_sidecar.rs:401` — NUL-byte refusal message contains two runs of ~22 literal spaces *Fix:* Re-wrap the literal with `\` line continuations like its neighbours. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: the refusal read `…no argv token may` + twenty-two spaces + `carry: …`, twice. The literal is wrapped with continuations, and the test that walks every malformed shape now holds each refusal to having no run of spaces.
- **R3-0116** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_sidecar.rs:776` — Spawn-failure remediation names environment variables the broker never reads *Fix:* Emit that sentence only when `principal` is None; on the distinct arm point at the config's `sidecar` block and the sudoers vector. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: under a distinct principal the advice told the operator to set `BROPS_GOVERNED_PYTHON` / `BROPS_GOVERNED_SIDECAR` — I searched the workspace: only the desktop app reads them, the broker does not. On that path it now points at the config's `sidecar` block and the sudoers vector for the account; the desktop path keeps the variables. Tested through a real failed spawn under a distinct principal, and the call site was broken once to see that test go red.
- **R3-0117** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_verification.rs:558` — Four comparisons in bind_attested_evidence can never fail when reached, and the run-identity tests cannot tell them from step 4b *Fix:* Say in the comment that (d) and the first half of (e) are implied, or drop them and keep one tested copy. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the comments say which comparisons of the attestation cannot be the refusing check when reached through `verify_envelope`, and which are not implied. A test calls the function directly so only those comparisons can refuse, six fields each with a control. It pins behaviour that already held; no mutant was run.
- **R3-0118** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/key_manifest.rs:433` — A directory-sync failure reports NotPersisted after the floor file has already been replaced *Fix:* Document the outcome as 'written, durability unknown' or return the advanced floor with a distinct error. **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (the message), not the return type** — REPRODUCED on a real directory this process may write but not read (mode 0300): the temp file and the rename succeed, the directory cannot be opened to be synced, and the refusal read `sync directory …: Permission denied` with the new floor already in place. It is still a refusal; the message and the variant's doc now say the file WAS replaced and only its durability is unknown. Returning the advanced floor with a distinct error would change what four callers do with a floor of unknown durability, and was not done. Unix only.
- **R3-0119** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/lib.rs:62` — now() swallows a clock error into "0", breaking the fixed-width ordering its doc promises *Fix:* Return a Result (or zero-pad and refuse a pre-epoch clock) instead of defaulting to 0. **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (the format), not a refusal** — an unreadable clock stamps `0000000000000`, the width the doc promises, instead of `"0"`. Measured on the way: the old `"0"` also sorted before every real timestamp, so no ordering was wrong in practice; what was false was the stated format. `now` still does not refuse — making it a `Result` reaches every caller and was not done.
- **R3-0120** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/local_write_record.rs:352` — check_chain reports Intact for a row whose operation/subject_kind is outside the domain when the original token equals map()'s default *Fix:* Give check_chain the same raw-token domain check record_break makes, or make map() return an error for an unknown token. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with record 2's `operation` rewritten to `blessed` (and, separately, its `subject_kind` to a foreign word), `check_chain` answered `Intact` — `map` put the default back, and the default was the original, so the hash recomputed. The whole-ledger check reads the stored tokens and reports the row as outside its domain, as `state_of` already did for one record. Each half of the check was removed once; the test went red both times.
- **R3-0121** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/receipt.rs:382` — BadSignatureLength reports base64 length as signature bytes *Fix:* Return a distinct oversize-wire error for the pre-decode cap. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a 200-character signature string was refused as `signature is 200 bytes, expected 64`. The pre-decode guard returns its own variant, which says base64 characters; the guard after decoding still counts bytes and says bytes. Diagnostics only — both remain a refusal.
- **R3-0122** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/receipt.rs:651` — TooLarge reports the base64 character count as the envelope's byte size *Fix:* Use a separate variant or message for the pre-decode length guard. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the envelope's pre-decode guard reported a character count as the envelope's size in bytes. It has its own variant and says characters. Shown by putting the old variant back.
- **R3-0123** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/receipt.rs:666` — Duplicate-key detection is in-band text matching on the serde error; other errors containing '__dup__' are misclassified *Fix:* Carry the duplicate key out of the visitor through a side channel (e.g. a Cell/thread-local or a custom error type) instead of parsing the message. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — MEASURED with a probe that was removed again: the envelope `"__dup__abc"`, a bare JSON string with no duplicate in it, is refused as `DuplicateKey("abc\", expected a flat JSON object with string values")`. So the finding is true, and it is a wrong LABEL on a refusal that is still a refusal. Carrying the key out of band means rewriting the strict parser's deserializer inside the receipt verifier, and that is audited code changed for a diagnostic. Recommendation: do it, but in its own pull request with the verifier's negative matrix re-run, not folded into a batch of low findings.
- **R3-0124** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/receipt.rs:706` — Timestamp validation accepts non-canonical integers ('+2000', '0002000') *Fix:* Require the string to equal its parsed value's to_string() (digits only, no sign, no leading zero). **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `requested_at = "+2000"` parsed as a valid receipt. A timestamp is ASCII digits with no sign and no leading zero, inside `u64`. The engine writes these with `str(int)`, which is that spelling.
- **R3-0125** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/receipt_store.rs:345` — MAX_REASON_BYTES is not applied to verification errors; attacker-sized strings reach the evidence row and the UI *Fix:* Apply bounded_reason inside insert_attempt/record (and record_pre_verification_block) rather than trusting each caller. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED AND MEASURED: a correctly signed envelope with one extra field whose name is 20 KiB produced a 20,502-byte reason in the evidence row and in the outcome returned to the caller; a 50,000-character transport reason given to `record_pre_verification_block` produced 50,016 bytes. Both entry points bound the reason themselves now, once, and hand the same bounded text to the row and to the caller. The other blocked branches carry text the store itself writes.
- **R3-0126** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/receipt_store.rs:798` — Nested-transaction refusal names verify_and_record_receipt whichever entry point was called *Fix:* Pass the caller's name into in_immediate_tx or word the message generically. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a nested `record_pre_verification_block` was refused as `verify_and_record_receipt must own its transaction`. The message names none of the three entry points.
- **R3-0127** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:871` — approve_confirmed reports every SQLite error as "not found" *Fix:* Map only QueryReturnedNoRows to NotFound and pass other errors through as CoreError::Sqlite. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with the `approvals` table gone, the confirmation said the approval was not found. Only `QueryReturnedNoRows` is not-found; any other error of the read is passed through.
- **R3-0128** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:1716` — knowledge::search is documented case-insensitive but SQLite LIKE folds case for ASCII only *Fix:* Narrow the doc to ASCII, or lowercase both sides in Rust before matching. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a note titled `Որոշումների մատյան` was not found by `որոշումների`, nor a Russian one by its lower-case word: SQLite `LIKE` folds ASCII only. The comparison is done in Rust with Unicode lower-casing, in Armenian, Russian and ASCII; a literal `%` or `_` in the query still matches only itself (there are no wildcards left to escape). `LIKE '%…%'` scanned every row too; this reads them instead.
- **R3-0129** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:1850` — memory::set_pinned writes no audit event, unlike create and delete *Fix:* Take an Actor and record `memory.pinned` in the same transaction. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `set_pinned` takes the actor and records `memory.pinned` or `memory.unpinned` in the transaction that makes the change; a pin that changes no row records nothing. The window's command passes the local operator, the seed passes the seed.
- **R3-0130** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2197` — A failed step execution, which has already spent a native-confirmed grant, writes no audit event *Fix:* Take an Actor and record a `run_step.failed` audit event in the same transaction. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-14)** — `fail_step_execution` fails the step and the run and records no audit row. Recommended: have it take an Actor and record `run_step.failed` in the same transaction, the caller passing `Actor::run_executor()`.
- **R3-0131** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2315` — set_step_status consumes an approval grant and completes a gated step with no audit record *Fix:* Give set_step_status an Actor and record a `run_step.status_changed` event in the transaction. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-14)** — `set_step_status` takes no Actor, consumes a grant and writes no audit row; only tests reach it. Recommended: do it with R3-0130, taking an Actor.
- **R3-0132** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2452` — advance() stamps a run terminal while another step is still `active` *Fix:* Before stamping a terminal status, check that no step is still `active` and return without terminating if one is. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with two steps active and none pending, one `advance` completed the gated step and returned the run as `succeeded` while the other step was still `active`. The run is finished only when no step is active either; the next advance completes the remaining step, with its own gate, and then finishes the run.
- **R3-0133** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2691` — automations::run commits the action before, and separately from, the run-log row that marks it as run *Fix:* Run execute_action and the automation_runs insert inside one atomic() call. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED AND MEASURED: with the run-log insert made to fail, three ticks left three notifications and no run row — the action was committed on its own, and `due` is decided from the log, so every tick did it again. The action and the row that says it ran are one transaction now (`execute_action`'s own `atomic` calls join it); the same three ticks leave nothing behind and each still reports the failure.
- **R3-0134** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2725` — parse_interval_ms panics on a trigger ending in a multi-byte character, inside the scheduler tick holding the DB mutex *Fix:* Split on the last char (`rest.chars().last()` / `char_indices`) instead of `len() - 1`, and add a non-ASCII test. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `K-03`: the unit is taken off with `strip_suffix`, never a byte index; `lib.rs` holds a table of multibyte triggers that must return `None`.
- **R3-0135** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2731` — parse_interval_ms multiplies without an overflow check *Fix:* Use checked_mul and return None on overflow. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — already `checked_mul`; `every: 9223372036854775807d` is tested to be `None`.
- **R3-0136** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2778` — A failing automations::list ends the tick before the agent half, contradicting the doc that both halves are always attempted *Fix:* Capture the list error into first_error and fall through to the agent half. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with the automation table unreadable and one produced agent due, the tick row read `(0, 0)` — the agent half was never attempted. The list's error is kept as the tick's first error and the agent half runs; the tick still fails and still names the table. The kept error was dropped once and the test went red.
- **R3-0137** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:3064` — enqueue_due slices `&digest[..12]` on an unvalidated digest *Fix:* Validate the digest as 64 lowercase hex characters in register/set_active, or use `digest.get(..12)`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `L-41`: a stored digest that is not 64 lowercase hex is a refused run before it is sliced or joined; seven malformed digests are tested.
- **R3-0138** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:3067` — A dropped duplicate flow run is still audited and counted as enqueued *Fix:* Put bundle_id in the run id and count/audit only when the INSERT changed a row. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: two bundles armed on one digest wrote ONE run row in a tick that audited and counted two. The run id carries the bundle as well as the digest, and a row that was not written is neither audited nor counted. The first version of the test did not reach the second rule (on a repeat tick the bundle is no longer due); a sweep showed it, and the test plants the colliding id now.
- **R3-0139** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:3094` — scheduler_ticks gets one row per minute and nothing ever prunes it *Fix:* Trim rows older than a fixed window inside record_tick. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — true: one row a minute, 525,600 a year by arithmetic (the size on disk was not measured), and nothing trims it. But the table exists so that an unattended scheduler can be audited, and how long that record is kept is a retention decision, not a bug fix. Recommendation: trim in `record_tick` to the last 90 days — long enough to audit a quarter, bounded at about 130,000 rows.
- **R3-0140** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:3140` — claim_and_run dispatches queued runs of a bundle that was disarmed *Fix:* Have claim_and_run require a matching agent_bundle_active row (or have disarm settle the bundle's queued runs as refused). **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a run queued while its bundle was armed, then the bundle disarmed, then the tick: the run finished `done`. Disarming is the stop button, so the claim now asks `agent_bundle_active` at the moment of dispatch and settles the run as refused with `bundle_not_armed`, writing nothing. The real artifact producer was run again from an empty directory afterwards and `check_produced_artifact.py` is GREEN. **Not tested:** two bundles sharing one digest with only one of them disarmed — the query names both the bundle and the digest, and no test separates them.
- **R3-0141** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:3201` — run_steps executes steps in array order and never reads the flow's `next` edges *Fix:* Walk the flow by following `next` from the first step (refusing a malformed graph), or refuse at verify any flow whose edges are not the array order.
- **R3-0142** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:3204` — A `store` step ignores its `verb` and always writes a knowledge note *Fix:* Match on the verb against a closed set and refuse an unknown one with a typed Refusal. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a store step whose verb was `notify` finished `done` and wrote a knowledge note. The executor implements one verb, `knowledge_note`, and compares it exactly; another verb, another case, an empty one or none is refused with a new typed reason, `store_verb_not_executable` (the suite counts the reasons, and the count was raised deliberately, 20 to 21). I did NOT implement `notify` or `task` for produced agents: that would widen what an unattended agent may write, which is a decision and not a bug fix. The field's doc said `the verb execute_action understands` — that is the automation vocabulary, a different one; corrected. The real artifact producer, whose flow uses `knowledge_note`, still passes its gate.
- **R3-0143** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:4528` — security::summary counts escalated approvals as neither pending nor decided *Fix:* Count `escalated` with pending, or add a third field for it. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: escalating one of five pending approvals left the summary with four pending and no more decided. `escalated` is counted as still awaiting a decision, which is what migration 0019 says it is.
- **R3-0144** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:4759` — The five seeded pending approvals can never be approved: they carry no nonce or request digest *Fix:* Seed these through approvals::create, or mark them as illustrative so the UI does not offer Approve on them.
- **R3-0145** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/supervisor_ledger.rs:808` — enqueue_terminal answers Idempotent on payload hash alone and takes run_id from the caller *Fix:* Compare every stored column on retry and read run_id from governed_turn_acceptance (the function is currently unreached). **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-14)** — `enqueue_terminal` compares only the payload digest on a retry and builds the filename from the caller's run id; the doc now says so. Reached by tests only. Recommended: decide R3-0693 first (delete the unreached acceptance/outbox half or wire it); if it stays, compare every stored column and take the run id from the acceptance row.
- **R3-0146** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/supervisor_ledger.rs:995` — The stored-row corruption check accepts a 64-character non-hex final_event_hash *Fix:* Apply the same hex test in refuse_corrupt that validate applies to the presented head. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a stored row whose `final_event_hash` was 64 `z`s was read back as intact. The stored value is held to the rule a presented head is. The sweep also found that rule's hex half held by no test for a PRESENTED head — the existing case was refused for its length — and it has one now.
- **R3-0147** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/launcher/src/main.rs:1028` — `drop_bounding_and_ambient_caps` ignores every PR_CAPBSET_DROP result but the step is journalled as completed *Fix:* Treat any errno other than EINVAL from PR_CAPBSET_DROP as failure and fix the comment to name the /proc/self/status check. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED with the real syscall, unprivileged: every `PR_CAPBSET_DROP` was `EPERM` and the step returned success. Each result is checked; `EINVAL` (a capability number this kernel lacks) still passes. The setuid-root path is exercised only by the live ladder in CI.
- **R3-0148** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/proof/src/bin/governed_recorder.rs:642` — Head-sequence counter silently restarts at 1 on any open error where `path.exists()` is false *Fix:* Decide 'missing' from the open's own errno (ENOENT only) instead of a second path lookup. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED on a real directory: with a dangling symlink where the counter should be, the sequence went 1, 2, then 1 again, written over the link. "Missing" is decided by an `lstat` that follows nothing; only `ENOENT` starts a counter. One arm of the new code — an `lstat` failing for another reason — is not held by a test and the code says so: the directory is already checked to be the recorder's own.
- **R3-0149** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/proof/src/bin/governed_recorder.rs:828` — `waitpid` result is unchecked; a failed wait reads as exit code 0 *Fix:* Check `waitpid(..) == pid` (retrying EINTR) and treat any other return as a refusal. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED with the real syscall: a `waitpid` on a pid that is not our child fails with `ECHILD`, leaves `status` at 0, and read as exit code 0. The wait's own result is read, `EINTR` is retried, anything else is a refusal before any output is published. **Not held by a test:** that `recorder()` returns on that refusal — the function is tested, the call site is three lines and was read.
- **R3-0150** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/proof/src/bin/ladder_turn.rs:218` — Evidence writers swallow every I/O error, so a run can exit 0 with no evidence on disk *Fix:* Return the write result and make `finish` exit non-zero when its own evidence document could not be written. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `finish` with a matching outcome and an evidence directory that does not exist returned 0. Both writers return their result; a failed write is printed and marked, and a marked run exits 1 whatever its outcome. Returning the result made the compiler name a third call site I had missed. Four mutations, four reds. **Not run here:** the live ladder itself — it needs root and the seven accounts; CI runs it.
- **R3-0151** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/proof/src/bin/ladder_turn.rs:780` — `manifest_sig_unreadable` reports the manifest path and hides a missing config key *Fix:* Split the lookup from the read, return `config_missing`/`trust.manifest_sig_path` for the first and pass the signature path as the detail for the sec… **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a config with no `trust.manifest_sig_path` was `manifest_sig_unreadable`, with the MANIFEST's path as the detail. A missing key is `config_missing: trust.manifest_sig_path`, like its neighbours; an unreadable file is `manifest_sig_unreadable` naming the signature file. I searched the scripts and workflows for the name: nothing outside the three drivers reads it. Three mutations, three reds.
- **R3-0152** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/anchor.rs:592` — A read-only filesystem makes every custody probe 'unmeasurable', so the app refuses on the least writable anchor *Fix:* Treat ReadOnlyFilesystem as a refusal of the write (recorded as such) rather than as unmeasurable. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — a read-only filesystem's refusal is a refusal of the write, by one classifier both probes use: the create in a directory and the open of an existing entry. The rule is tested as a function; NOT run against a read-only mount, which this account cannot make. The first push of its text check failed on `windows-latest`: the marker was written with `\n` and the checkout there is CRLF. It normalises line endings now.
- **R3-0153** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/lib.rs:1033` — Argument-validation failures are reported as a corrupt trust store *Fix:* Add an InvalidArgument variant for caller input and keep Corrupt for on-disk state. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a head sequence of zero came back as `Corrupt`, whose message says the trust store is not usable and talks about moving the `trust` directory. The three refusals that are about the caller's arguments (a non-positive head sequence, an expiry in the past, an empty id) return `InvalidArgument`, which says the request cannot be done and that the store was not touched. Each site's old refusal was put back once; the test went red each time. I searched the workspace for anything outside the crate that matches on the error's variants and found only constructions.
- **R3-0154** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/lib.rs:1436` — The idempotency and occupancy checks use is_file()/exists(), which answer false for every error — the defect this file fixed for the custody record *Fix:* Use symlink_metadata and classify only NotFound as absent, as custody_record_from_read and settle_existing do. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the five `is_file()` / `exists()` checks go through one decision function, tested apart from the probe as `custody_record_from_read` is: with the old behaviour in it, a `PermissionDenied` probe answered `Absent` (red); now only `NotFound` is absence and every other failure is an error naming the path. The probe does not follow a link, so a linked manifest is no longer taken for the idempotency marker. **Not staged end to end:** on this box a denied anchor directory is also refused later for other reasons (the trust path is occupied, or the write fails), so the early refusal is shown through the decision function, not through a real mint.
- **R3-0155** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/lib.rs:2043` — The 'keys directory holds the retained authorities and nothing else' check accepts extension-less names and directories *Fix:* Require the name to end in .json, be a regular file, and be exactly one of the manifest-covered key files. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED for the extension-less name: a second file called exactly an authority's name was accepted as that authority. The name must be `<authority>.json`. The DIRECTORY half of the finding is not reachable: a directory under a key file's name is refused earlier, when the store is re-hashed (`Is a directory`) — my first test used one and showed me that. What IS reachable, and was accepted, is a LINK under a key file's name pointing at a copy of the right bytes: the re-hash follows it. An entry must be a regular file, asked of the entry itself. Each half was removed once; a named test went red both times.
- **R3-0156** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/lib.rs:2169` — verify_store never reads the anti-rollback floor file it hands to the engine *Fix:* Read the floor in verify_store and require it to equal the registry payload's registry_version.
- **R3-0157** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/posix_install.rs:240` — refuse_ambiguous_path accepts repeated separators, so the installer binds a spelling the application never computes *Fix:* Refuse any path whose string differs from its component-rebuilt form. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `/home//gev/x` and a trailing `/` were accepted. The raw string is judged segment by segment; a repeated or trailing separator is refused.
- **R3-0158** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/posix_install.rs:247` — refuse_ambiguous_path can never refuse a `.` component: Path::components() normalises it away on an absolute path *Fix:* Check the raw string (split on '/' and refuse any "." or empty segment) instead of relying on components(). **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `/home/gev/./x` was accepted, because `components()` drops the `.` before the check sees it. Same change as `R3-0157`.
- **R3-0159** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/tests/posix_install.rs:796` — Desktop-account fallback skips $SUDO_USER when BROPS_TEST_DESKTOP_USER is set but empty or 'root' *Fix:* Apply the non-empty/non-root filter to each source before or_else, so a rejected first value falls through to $SUDO_USER. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `BROPS_TEST_DESKTOP_USER=""` with `SUDO_USER=bob` chose `nobody`. The non-empty/non-root filter is asked of each source. A test's own fixture; the root test that uses it was not run here.
- **R3-0160** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:599` — claude-cli readiness is `--version` succeeding, but the status text speaks about login *Fix:* Word the detail as "binary found (login unverified)", as the Anthropic arm does for its key. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: the sentence called the binary `available` and blamed a failed probe on being `not logged in`; the probe is `--version` and knows neither. It says the binary was found and that its login is not checked here, and on failure that it was not found or did not answer `--version`. The sentence was moved into a function unchanged first, so the test could be red on the old wording.
- **R3-0161** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:638` — The Ollama status discards the validation error and always blames a non-local host *Fix:* Keep the Err string from validate_ollama_url and put it in the detail. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a URL refused for its port, its path or its scheme was each reported as `must be a local host`. The status carries the validator's own reason. Same method: moved unchanged, red, then changed.
- **R3-0162** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:732` — Stop does nothing for the Anthropic and Ollama providers *Fix:* Race the HTTP future against the cancel flag (poll it as the CLI loop does) and return the cancelled result.
- **R3-0163** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1419` — Every agent turn creates an empty quarantine directory even when nothing changed *Fix:* Create the quarantine directory lazily, on the first file that needs it. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: asking where rejected content would go created the directory. It is a name now and appears with the first file kept in it. Found on the way: NO test read the kept copy — the new one reads the turn's bytes back out of the directory the notice names — and the notice named the directory even when nothing reached it; it does so only after a successful write. Three mutations, three reds.
- **R3-0164** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1487` — The guard restores bytes but not the file mode, and does not detect a mode change *Fix:* Record the unix mode in FileSnapshot, compare it, and re-apply it on restore. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a turn that made a protected file world-writable, bytes untouched, produced no notice about it; and a deleted executable came back at the default mode. The snapshot holds each file's permission bits; a mode change is a change, is put back, and the notice names both modes. A restored file gets its mode back with its bytes. Unix only: there are no mode bits to read on Windows.
- **R3-0165** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2179` — clean_path_token strips in the wrong order, so a backticked path followed by a period is rejected *Fix:* Trim trailing punctuation first, then the quote characters. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `` scope: `apps/desktop/src`. `` read as no scope. Quotes and trailing punctuation are peeled until neither is left.
- **R3-0166** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2235` — A Markdown-bold label (`**scope:** path`) yields no scope *Fix:* Strip leading emphasis characters from the remainder after the label as well. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `**scope:** path` read as no scope. The closing `**` is skipped when it stands alone before whitespace, and only then (`*.md` keeps its star). Shown by removing the skip.
- **R3-0167** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2255` — One prose token on a later scope line erases paths already taken from earlier lines *Fix:* Collect each line into a local vector and append it to the target only if every token passed. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a later prose line erased an earlier line's paths. A line's paths are taken or dropped together and never reach back. Shown by restoring the clear. **Seen while writing the test, not fixed:** a line of all-lowercase prose (`do not touch it`) is read as four paths, before this change and after it — `tools` and `do` are the same shape to `is_grant_path`. It is a limit of reading a grant out of text; the card already says the grant is stated, not enforced.
- **R3-0168** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2533` — Stop settles the trust surface before the killed child has exited *Fix:* Await child.wait() (bounded by a short timeout) before calling settle on the cancel path.
- **R3-0169** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2786` — The non-streaming CLI path gives an agent-mode turn 120 s while the streaming path gives it 900 s *Fix:* Use the same agent-aware deadline in claude_cli. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with both budgets put behind one function at their old values, the agent's was 120 s on the non-streaming path and 900 s on the streaming one. An agent turn gets 900 s on either; a conversational turn keeps 180 and 120. **Not tested:** that `claude_cli` passes its own agent flag to that function — the function is tested, the one call site is read (`project_dir.is_some()`, the same expression that already decides the agent's tools there).
- **R3-0170** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:3205` — A base URL with a trailing slash passes validation and then produces a double-slash endpoint *Fix:* Trim a trailing '/' from the URL before appending the endpoint. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — both call sites join the endpoint through `ollama_endpoint`, which drops the base's trailing slashes; the URL that was built is shown by the test. What an Ollama server does with `//api/chat` was not measured — none runs here.
- **R3-0171** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1408` — The pre-flight measures which inputs are missing and then reports a fixed 'provisions NONE' reason *Fix:* Build the durable reason from the measured `missing` list so a partially provisioned install is described truthfully. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED (with the reason moved into a function at its old behaviour): an install lacking two of the five inputs was told it `provisions NONE`. The fixed sentence is used only when all five are absent — every shipped build today; a partial list gets a reason naming what is missing and nothing else. The decision to refuse is untouched: it is still `missing` being non-empty. Four mutations, four reds.
- **R3-0172** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1559` — conversation_turn_context swallows two database errors into silent fallbacks *Fix:* Return the error; the function already returns Result<_, String>. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with the agent table unreadable the turn was assembled and attributed to `Bro`; with the roster unreadable it was assembled as an empty room. Both errors are returned, so a failed read fails the turn instead of changing the system prompt whose hash the receipt binds. Each `?` was put back once; the test went red both times.
- **R3-0173** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1605` — hidden_ask_conversation turns a failed read into 'none exists' and creates another *Fix:* Propagate the list error with `?` instead of `.ok()`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED (with the old `.ok()` put back): an `ask` row that could not be decoded made the lookup return a NEW conversation, leaving two. A failed read is an error now and creates nothing.
- **R3-0174** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2058` — Moving the run to awaiting_approval discards its error *Fix:* Propagate the error (and create the approval and the status change in one transaction).
- **R3-0175** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2127` — fail_attempt! swallows both the lock failure and the fail_step_execution result *Fix:* Report a failure to settle the attempt in the emitted error instead of discarding it. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED (with the settling moved into a function at its old behaviour): an attempt the store refused to fail — a stale attempt id — and one with no connection at all both returned the turn's reason alone, the step still claimed. The reason is passed on unchanged when the attempt was settled; when it was not, the message says so and why. **Not tested:** the macro's two-line call into that function inside the Tauri command, which needs a window and a channel.
- **R3-0176** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2179` — Post-turn error returns in stream_run_step leave the step claimed with no failure recorded *Fix:* Route these returns through fail_attempt! (or an equivalent) so the claim is settled. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: with the run unreadable, and with the result unwritable (a trigger refusing `done`), the function returned its error and the step stayed `active`. Every refusal before the result is written fails the claim; a failed `advance` AFTER the result is written is reported and the step stays done. Five mutations across this and R3-0175, five reds. Measured on the way: a claim left behind is failed at the next start (`src/lib.rs` calls `reconcile_abandoned_executions`). **Not tested:** the command's own lock-failure arm.
- **R3-0177** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2551` — delete_automation's changed-while-open check ignores the enabled state the dialog displayed *Fix:* Include `enabled` in the comparison (or compare the whole prompt body). **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — already closed by #388 (`88b4d1d`, audit `K-06`): the check compares the dialog as it would be drawn now with the dialog that was shown, so `enabled` is compared because it is displayed. MEASURED today: with that comparison removed, `an_automation_armed_or_disarmed_while_the_dialog_was_open_is_not_deleted` goes red.
- **R3-0178** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2809` — demonstration_verified_reply prompts the model as Bro and then attributes the reply to the requested agent *Fix:* Resolve the author before building the prompt and name that author in it, as conversation_turn_context does.
- **R3-0179** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/files.rs:431` — write_text overwrites a file the user made read-only *Fix:* Refuse (and report readonly) when the original's permissions are read-only for the owner. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a file made read-only was replaced through the temporary-file rename. `write_text` refuses a read-only file before the temporary exists; nothing is left beside it.
- **R3-0180** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governance.rs:126` — bounded() has a dead condition and deletes line breaks instead of replacing them *Fix:* Map control characters to a space, then collapse, instead of filtering them out. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `lease refused\nno grant on file` came out as `lease refusedno grant on file`. Control characters become a space and whitespace collapses; the dead half of the old condition is gone with it.
- **R3-0181** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_selftest.rs:142` — The self-test waits on the model CLI with no timeout, inside a synchronous Tauri command *Fix:* Bound the wait and kill the child on expiry, reporting BuiltinPlaceholderModelFailed, and run the command off the main thread. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `K-05` (2026-10-09): the self-test runs no model and spawns no process on any platform.
- **R3-0182** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_selftest.rs:182` — Self-test reports trust_state "trusted_verified" for a row that committed as "demonstration_custody" *Fix:* Return the committed label the chain actually wrote (carry it on ProofOutcome) instead of a hardcoded string. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: the command returned `trusted_verified` for a run whose durable row says `demonstration_custody` (the test that pinned the wrong label was changed first and went red: left `trusted_verified`, right `demonstration_custody`). `ProofOutcome` carries the label read from the committed row, and the self-test returns that. The renderer does not print the label, so nothing on screen changes; the wire no longer says production.
- **R3-0183** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_turn.rs:224` — governed_turn_execute is a synchronous Tauri command that can block for the whole 120 s exchange budget *Fix:* Make it `#[tauri::command(async)]` or run the exchange under spawn_blocking.
- **R3-0184** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_turn.rs:263` — The connect itself has no deadline, so the 'never a wedged command' bound starts only after it returns *Fix:* Connect non-blocking with a deadline, or start the budget before the connect.
- **R3-0185** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:117` — retire_orphaned_anchor treats another OS account's valid machine-wide anchor as an uninstall leftover *Fix:* Decide orphanhood from the anchor manifest's bound trust path, not from the current account's data directory.
- **R3-0186** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-broker/proof/isolation_proof.ps1:240` — Server output is redirected into `$Dir\out\` which the script never creates and the prereqs never mention *Fix:* Create the directory before the first case.
- **R3-0187** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-broker/src/bin/pipe_proof.rs:83` — Security-descriptor setup errors are discarded before the pipe is created *Fix:* Propagate both results as PROOF_ERROR.
- **R3-0188** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-broker/src/bin/spawn_as.rs:221` — SetEntriesInAclW failure is reported from GetLastError instead of its return code *Fix:* Build the error from `rc`.
- **R3-0189** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/WIRING_LIVE_TRUST.md:63` — Runbook order is wrong: step 1 provisions with the executor path that step 2 builds *Fix:* Put the build (including win_provision) first. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the runbook built the kit in step 2 and ran `win_provision` with `--executor-path` in step 1. - **R3-0195** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/bin/win_provision.rs:381` — run_id, task_id and requested_at are provisioning-time constants signed into every turn of the deployment *Fix:* Mint run_id/task_id and read requested_at in the driver per turn; keep only workspace/install ids in config.

- **R3-0198** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/head_sequence.rs:157` — The 'durable' counter never syncs its claim marker, its hint or the directory *Fix:* sync_all the marker and the staged hint, and fsync the directory where the platform allows, before returning the number.

- **R3-0200** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/resolver.rs:164` — Windows persist_floor is the fs::write + rename with no sync that core's write_floor_atomically was rewritten to remove *Fix:* Write the signed floor through brops_core::key_manifest::write_floor_atomically.

- **R3-0203** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/servers.rs:428` — The evidence-chain size bound is applied after the whole file has been read into memory *Fix:* Check metadata().len() or read through a `take(1<<20 + 1)` reader before allocating.

A decision's pill takes the tone of the family `Decisions.status.ts` already puts its row in, and a test holds the two together; `consumed` and `escalated` have tones too. Visible change: a `proposed` decision's pill is amber, as its row already was.
- **R3-0218** `apps/desktop/src/domain/statusLabels.ts:10` — Declared statuses `expired`, `open` and `superseded` have no label and render as English in hy/ru *Fix:* Add the three entries with hy/ru values, and a test that every declared status has a label. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `open`, `superseded` and `expired` read as English in Armenian and Russian. They have labels, with `consumed` and `escalated`; a test holds every declared status to a tone and to a label in all three languages.
- **R3-0219** `apps/desktop/src/features/Activity.tsx:133` — Rate is floored at 1/min, contradicting 'every number shown is DERIVED' *Fix:* Show the real quotient (e.g. '<1' or a per-hour unit) instead of clamping to 1. **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — REPRODUCED: three events across an hour were announced as `About 1 per minute`. The rate is the quotient; below one a minute it reads `<1`, on screen and to a screen reader, and a rate above one is still rounded.
- **R3-0220** `apps/desktop/src/features/Approvals.strings.ts:162` — Two Armenian queue labels mean something different from the English *Fix:* Translate as «Թիրախ» and «Սպասում». **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — «Թիրախ» and «Սպասում»; the two heads read «Ազդեցություն» (impact) and «Ժամկետ» (deadline).
- **R3-0221** `apps/desktop/src/features/Approvals.tsx:355` — After `g`/`d`/`e`, Enter cancels instead of confirming, because Modal puts focus on Cancel *Fix:* Give the confirm button initial focus (autoFocus) or drop the window-level Enter branch and state that Enter acts on the focused button. **NOT CHANGED, deliberately — 2026-10-09.** Read and confirmed: `ConfirmDialog` renders Cancel first, `Modal` focuses the first button, so `g` then Enter cancels. That is the safe default on an approval gate, and making Enter confirm a grant is a decision about the gate, not a bug fix. What WAS false is fixed: the comment above the handler said `Enter commits`. Recommendation for the Owner: keep it — one extra Tab before an approval is cheap, and a deny is immediate and irreversible. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — the comment that said `Enter commits` is already corrected in the code, which now states what happens: the dialog opens with focus on Cancel, so `g` then Enter cancels. Whether a grant, a deny or an escalation should be confirmable by one more Enter is a decision about a gate, and it is his. Recommendation: leave it as it is — the key that stages a decision should not also be one keystroke from committing it, and a deny is immediate and irreversible.
- **R3-0222** `apps/desktop/src/features/Approvals.tsx:723` — Every risk level in the queue row is painted with the success tone *Fix:* Choose the class from the risk level, or drop the tone class. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — REPRODUCED: `high`, `critical` and `medium` rows each carried `q-rev ok`. The tone follows the level — `ok` for low, `no` for high and critical, none for anything else, because `.q-rev` has only those two.
- **R3-0223** `apps/desktop/src/features/Automations.tsx:572` — Every rejected run_automation, including a plain breakdown, is recorded in the history as 'refused by the store' *Fix:* Record a store refusal only when isDenial(message) holds and record other rejections as a failed entry. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a `run_automation` rejected with `database is locked` was written into the run history as `refused by the store`. Only a refusal is recorded there; a breakdown is still said, with its reason.
- **R3-0224** `apps/desktop/src/features/Automations.tsx:729` — A lane with an empty trigger is announced as 'any event' *Fix:* Use describeTrigger(a.trigger, L).text (or the manual-only string) in the accessible name. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a conduit with an empty trigger had the accessible name `…, any event, Armed`. An empty trigger is the manual one, and it says so; the `anyEvent` string, which nothing else used, is deleted.
- **R3-0225** `apps/desktop/src/features/Automations.tsx:957` — 'No runs yet' is printed before the run history has been read *Fix:* Render nothing (or a loading note) while runs.loading is true. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — REPRODUCED with a history read that never answers: the sentence was on screen. It is printed only when the read has answered with no rows; an empty history that did come back still says it.
- **R3-0226** `apps/desktop/src/features/Automations.tsx:1235` — .au-warn references an undeclared token and always falls back to danger red *Fix:* Use var(--warning). **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — `var(--warning)`. A test now reads every stylesheet, in `.css` or inside a component, and refuses a `var()` whose token is declared nowhere and so can only ever be its fallback; it was red on this one and on eight more.
- **R3-0227** `apps/desktop/src/features/Bridge.tsx:39` — Three CSS tokens the panel uses are declared nowhere *Fix:* Use the declared tokens (--line / --raised / --r). **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — `--line`, `--raised`, `--r`. The same check found the same three misnamed in `Decisions.tsx`, `Notifications.tsx` and `ui.css`, and `--warn-rgb` in `Tasks.tsx` (a warning note that was drawn in the line grey) — all renamed to the declared tokens. Seven names that ARE set at runtime and four knobs nobody sets are allowed by name, each checked. Real Chromium 434 passed, contrast 98 pairs.
- **R3-0228** `apps/desktop/src/features/Bridge.tsx:165` — A post-send transport failure is rendered as 'No broker allowed or refused this turn' *Fix:* Give broker_transport_failed (and unclassified_transport_failure) the 'no verdict received, the broker may have decided' wording rather than outcomeU… **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — REPRODUCED: `broker_transport_failed` and an unclassified failure were both told `No verdict exists — no broker allowed or refused this turn`. They read `No verdict received`, and that this does not establish the broker decided nothing; a broker that was never reached still says nobody decided (the control). Three languages. **CORRECTED 2026-10-09, later the same day — the fix above was wrong for the commonest case.** The first real install showed what a send actually returns on a shipped build: `Command governed_turn_execute not allowed by ACL`. That matched no named kind, so it was `unclassified_transport_failure`, and the wording just given to that kind told the reader the request `may have reached the broker`. It never ran. It is its own kind now, `command_not_allowed`, with the nobody-decided words and `never left the desktop`; a test is driven by the string the installed app printed.
- **R3-0229** `apps/desktop/src/features/Bridge.tsx:187` — Verdict mirror shows the previous decision's record count under the newly selected decision *Fix:* Pass established(verdicts) (or null while loading) to MirrorRow so a count is never shown for a taskId it was not read for. **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — REPRODUCED: `records · 2` from decision d-1 stood under d-2 while its read was in flight. The row is given `established(verdicts)` and says it is reading.
- **R3-0230** `apps/desktop/src/features/Bridge.tsx:216` — The panel's accessible name is hard-coded English *Fix:* Use aria-label={L('panelTitle')}. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — `aria-label={L('panelTitle')}`; red first in `hy`.
- **R3-0231** `apps/desktop/src/features/Bridge.tsx:222` — As a routed page the bridge has no h1 *Fix:* Wrap the routed use in a page header with an h1, keeping h2 for the embedded use. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — MEASURED in Chromium over every routed page in two states: 44 of 46 had exactly one `<h1>`, and the bridge had none in both. As a routed page the panel's title is its `<h1>`; embedded under Decisions it stays an `<h2>`, and the two compute the same size (22.5px, what the `<h2>` was before). The sweep stays, so the next page without a heading — or with two — is found by it. **My first version of this was red in CI** (`a11y`, axe `heading-order`): with the title an `<h1>`, the panel's two `<h3>` sections skipped a level. They are `<h2>` on the routed page and `<h3>` embedded. I had not run `npm run test:a11y` locally — it is not in the chain I run — and CI is where I learned it.
- **R3-0232** `apps/desktop/src/features/Calendar.tsx:55` — Event kinds are coloured from the task/run status table *Fix:* Give event kinds their own tone map with a neutral default. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: an event whose kind was `review` was amber and one called `blocked` red, from the task status table. Event kinds have their own tones: the five kinds the app has labels for keep the accent every ordinary event had, anything else is neutral. Visible change: an event of a kind the app has no label for is muted where it was cyan.
- **R3-0233** `apps/desktop/src/features/Calendar.tsx:205` — Run-history times bypass the shared parser and the page locale *Fix:* Format with parseTimestamp(run.ranAt) and the page's locale, with a dash for null. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — REPRODUCED: an ISO time printed `Invalid Date`. Times go through `parseTimestamp` and the page's locale, and an unreadable one is a dash.
- **R3-0234** `apps/desktop/src/features/Command.tsx:87` — A failed or skipped step is styled as a waiting one *Fix:* Add explicit failed/skipped cases with their own classes. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — REPRODUCED: `failed` and `skipped` both carried `rs-wait`. They carry `rs-fail` and `rs-skip`, with rules in `aios.css`; contrast and token gates green, real Chromium 434 passed.
- **R3-0235** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:390` — Mentioned agents do not answer 'in the order named' *Fix:* Sort the matches by their index in the body, or correct the comment. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — same function: the result is sorted by where each name appears in the message, so the order named is the order answered. Red first.
- **R3-0236** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:394` — Mention routing uses \b, which is not a boundary after a non-ASCII agent name *Fix:* Use a Unicode-aware lookahead such as `(?![\p{L}\p{N}_])` with the `u` flag. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — REPRODUCED with the old pattern moved unchanged into a function: `@Գևորգ,`, `@Գևորգ.` and `@Анна!` routed to nobody. `mentionedAgents` ends a name with `(?![\p{L}\p{N}_])` under the `u` flag. Writing it found a second defect before it shipped: an escaped hyphen outside a class is a SyntaxError under `u`, so a name like `code-audit` would have thrown on send; only syntax characters are escaped, and a hyphen, a slash and a space in a name are each tested.
- **R3-0237** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:566` — The message log is labelled 'Conversations', the same name as the conversation rail *Fix:* Label the log with the conversation title or a 'Messages' string. **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — the log is named `Messages` (`chat.messages`, three languages); red first.
- **R3-0238** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:656` — The '@' mention button carries the 'Reply as' accessible name of the agent select *Fix:* Give the button and the listbox their own 'Mention an agent' label. **CLOSED 2026-10-09** — the button is named `Mention an agent` (`chat.mentionAgent`, three languages); red first.
- **R3-0239** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:823` — A failed roster write is 'surfaced' only to the console *Fix:* Show the failure in the workspace (the createError strip already exists) instead of the console. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: the roster write failed and the only trace was `console.error`. The page says the room was created and its participants were not saved, with the reason; starting another room takes the statement away. **Found beside it, not in the read, and fixed:** `FormRow` is a `<label>`, and 3 of its 47 uses wrapped buttons — a label forwards a click on its text to its first control, so clicking the word `Dependencies` in the task form called `remove_task_dependency` (reproduced), and the first button in each such row was named by the whole row. `FormRow` has a `group` form, the three rows use it, and a test scans every `FormRow` in the tree so the next one is refused.
