The hero heading is an `<h2>`; its one scoped style rule moved with it. The appearance is unchanged by my reading of the stylesheet (every property the two elements differ in by default is set by that rule or by the shared `h1,h2,h3` rule) — read, not measured in a browser; the Chromium specs pass but none names this heading.
- **R3-0272** `apps/desktop/src/features/Tasks.tsx:795` — The draft-warning band uses a token that is defined nowhere, so it always falls back to the neutral line colour *Fix:* Use `--warning-rgb`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — already closed by #374 (`c1c5e0f`, the nine style tokens that were only their fallback): the band reads `--warning-rgb`, which both themes define. Checked in the file today.
- **R3-0273** `apps/desktop/src/features/approvalsAuthority.ts:220` — `canAct`'s grant check can never be false, and the page never reads the command table *Fix:* Either read `granted` from the capability file at test time only and drop it from `canAct`, or route the page's calls through the table. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — `granted`, true in every entry, is removed and `canAct` reads `actionable` alone; the truth table is the same. The page still does not read the table, and a comment says so.
- **R3-0274** `apps/desktop/src/features/automationsGovernance.ts:193` — parseIntervalMs is not equivalent to the Rust parser it claims to port: `every: +5m` fires in Rust and reads 'unrecognised' here *Fix:* Make one side match the other (reject a sign in Rust, or accept it here) and add a shared fixture both suites read. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED, and wider than reported: one fixture now read by BOTH suites showed `every: +5m` firing in Rust, and three cases the page called scheduled that the scheduler ignores — a trigger wrapped in U+FEFF (JavaScript's `trim` strips it, Rust's does not), and a count whose interval overflows `i64`; plus one the other way, U+0085, which Rust trims and JavaScript does not. Rust takes digits only; the renderer trims Rust's measured whitespace set (the two sets were enumerated and compared, identical) and bounds the product at `i64::MAX`.
- **R3-0275** `apps/desktop/src/features/automationsGovernance.ts:453` — A stored run of a model-reaching verb is classified as an unknown verb, the distinction the module says it must keep *Fix:* Capture the verb from the store message and return `action_reaches_model` when it is in MODEL_REACHING_VERBS. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a stored `unknown action verb 'ask'` row was `action_verb_unknown`. The verb is read out of the store's sentence and a model-reaching one is `action_reaches_model`, as the pre-flight says.
- **R3-0276** `apps/desktop/src/features/delegationView.tsx:192` — A CLI built-in or unrecognised agent is labelled 'PACK ROLE' on the delegation card *Fix:* Derive the label from `agentOrigin` (tier / pack role / outside the model / unstated) instead of from `tier` alone. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a `cli_builtin` agent was labelled `PACK ROLE`. The label is derived from the stated origin; an unstated origin claims neither unless the name is itself a tier. An existing test asserted the wrong label for a CLI built-in and is corrected.
- **R3-0277** `apps/desktop/src/features/integrationsProbe.ts:183` — parseDeclareReply drops `authRef` from the record it returns *Fix:* Copy `authRef` through when it is a string or null. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a reply with `authRef: 'keyring:slack-bot'` came back as a record without it. The reference is copied through; one that is neither a string nor `null` refuses the record.
- **R3-0278** `apps/desktop/src/features/researchLibraryDeleteRefusal.ts:72` — isPolicyRefusal is wider than its docstring: untied prefix match and bare 'denied'/'forbidden' *Fix:* Require `forbidden_command:${command}:` as a prefix and drop the bare 'forbidden|denied' alternatives. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: another command's `forbidden_command:` refusal, the prefix quoted in the middle of an unrelated message, and `permission denied (os error 13)` were each a permanent policy verdict. The handler refusal must START with `forbidden_command:<this command>:`, and only the ACL's own wording counts beside it. The module had no unit test; it has one.
- **R3-0279** `apps/desktop/src/services/agentsDispatch.ts:461` — validateAssignment does not refuse duplicate `inputs`, which the schema forbids *Fix:* Add the same dupes() check for d.inputs. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `inputs` is held to `uniqueItems`, as the two scope lists already were. Shown by putting the old code back: a duplicate input then passes the pre-flight.
- **R3-0280** `apps/desktop/src/services/agentsDispatch.ts:475` — Empty-string done_criteria / verification / rollback commands pass pre-flight though the schema requires minLength 1 *Fix:* Refuse any entry that is empty after trim in done_criteria, verification.commands and rollback.commands. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — an empty `done_criteria`, `verification.commands` or `rollback.commands` entry is refused, as the task contract's `minLength: 1` says. Shown by removing each of the three checks in turn. (My first run of these tests was red for the wrong reason — it set the fields on the wrong object — and proved nothing; the type-check caught it.)
- **R3-0281** `apps/desktop/src/services/agentsDispatch.ts:720` — A refused reply is not correlated with the request; only an accepted one is *Fix:* Apply the client_request_id comparison to refused frames as well. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a `refused` frame carrying another request's id was returned as this request's refusal. It is `unreachable`, by the same comparison an accepted frame gets.
- **R3-0282** `apps/desktop/src/services/approvalRequest.ts:94` — A `recorded` outcome with no request_id is accepted with an empty id *Fix:* Return blocked when request_id is absent or empty. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a `recorded` outcome with no `request_id` was returned with `requestId: ''`. It is blocked, by a reason that names the missing id.
- **R3-0283** `apps/desktop/src/services/governedTurn.ts:128` — parseResult accepts frames its own schema refuses (extra properties) *Fix:* Reject any key outside the closed set at frame and message level, with a test per level. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a committed frame with a `note`, a message with an `html`, and a blocked frame with a `detail` were each returned as a typed result. Both frames and the message are held to the key sets `bridge/contracts/renderer-governed-turn-result.schema.json` closes.
- **R3-0284** `apps/desktop/src/services/governedTurn.ts:139` — created_at_ms accepts a non-integer; the schema says integer *Fix:* Use Number.isSafeInteger(createdAtMs) and drop the redundant `o.message &&`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `created_at_ms: 1700.5` parsed. It must be a safe integer. The contract sets no minimum and the parser invents none.
- **R3-0285** `apps/desktop/src/test/contrast.ts:118` — Ancestor opacity is applied to the text only, over an undimmed background *Fix:* Composite each opacity-bearing ancestor's whole subtree (text and background) over its backdrop, or document and test the limitation. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — an element's own opacity dims its background with its text. A white-on-black chip at half opacity over white was reported as 5.28:1 and is 3.98:1. No threshold or pair changed; the page sweep is still green.
- **R3-0286** `apps/desktop/src/theme/aios.css:316` — aios.css `.grid` overrides ui.css `.grid-3`/`.grid-2`, so TileGroup is never three columns *Fix:* Rename one of the two `.grid` utilities or raise `.grid-2/.grid-3` to `.grid.grid-3`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, nothing changed in the stylesheets. In Chromium at 1440px a `TileGroup` of four tiles computes three tracks: the two rules have equal specificity and `ui.css` arrives after `aios.css` (`main.tsx` imports `styles.css` first, `ui.css` comes in with `ui.tsx`), so `.grid-3` wins. With the `.grid-3` rule removed the same test reads four 338px tracks, so the measurement can go red; it stays in `ui.browser.spec.tsx`, because what it shows is that the layout rests on import order. My own first run was red for a third reason — the spec frame is phone-wide and I was measuring the 720px breakpoint.
- **R3-0287** `apps/desktop/src/theme/aios.css:342` — The hover lift on `.surface` cannot show on any surface that also carries `.reveal`/`.rise` *Fix:* Animate `translate`/opacity in `reveal` instead of `transform`, or drop `forwards` and set the end state in the base rule.
- **R3-0288** `apps/desktop/src/theme/aios.css:405` — `pill danger` is still applied and still has no rule; the comment says it was folded away *Fix:* Change Command.tsx to `bad`, or add `.pill.danger` beside `.pill.bad`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the failed-dispatch pill is `pill bad`, which has a rule. Shown by putting `danger` back: a test that holds the pill's tone class to a rule in the stylesheet goes red.
- **R3-0289** `apps/desktop/src/theme/aios.css:2916` — Integrations group headers reserve room for a dot whose colour variable is never set *Fix:* Delete the aios `.creg-gname` rules, or give `--gtc` a fallback.

- **R3-1333** `apps/desktop/src/features/filesModel.test.ts:5` — BACKEND_ERRORS is a hand-typed copy that is neither complete nor read from files.rs, so a backend wording change cannot fail it *Fix:* Read files.rs in the test (as Automations.governance.test.ts reads the Rust source) and assert every literal Err string is classified. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the test reads the Err strings out of `files.rs` and requires the table to be exactly that set; two were missing.
- **R3-1334** `apps/desktop/src/features/liveIndicators.guard.test.ts:19` — Both source-scanning guards glob one directory level, so nested components are never scanned *Fix:* Use `**/*.tsx` and exclude tests explicitly. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — both guards read every depth; `components/charts` was outside the one-level glob.
- **R3-1335** `apps/desktop/src/features/liveIndicators.guard.test.ts:29` — The guard cannot see a hard-coded `<LiveMark state="live">`, the library's own live primitive *Fix:* Add a `<LiveMark\s+state="live"` pattern (and a class-list pattern that tolerates extra classes) with a planted-literal control test. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the scan reads `state` in any position and either quoting, on `Mark` and `LiveMark`, and a class literal as a token set.
- **R3-1336** `apps/desktop/src/features/liveIndicators.guard.test.ts:68` — An allow-listed file may add any number of new hard-coded literals *Fix:* Store the expected count per file and assert equality. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the allow-list is a count per file and pattern; a second literal in an allowed file fails.
- **R3-1337** `apps/desktop/src/features/pages.a11y.spec.tsx:56` — The 'with data' axe pass samples after the first invoke plus one microtask, the settle the sibling spec documents as a measured flake *Fix:* Share one settle helper that polls until a populated fixture value is rendered, and use it in all three page sweeps. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the with-data passes wait until a row's value is on the screen, in jsdom and in Chromium, through one shared helper; with answers delayed 20 ms the old settle handed axe a page with no row.
- **R3-1338** `apps/desktop/src/features/pages.a11y.spec.tsx:79` — The jsdom axe project has no positive control: nothing shows axe can report a violation there *Fix:* Add one test that renders an unlabeled <img>/<button> and asserts axe reports it. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — a test plants an unnamed button and an image with no alt and requires axe to report both; nothing had shown the pass could fail.
- **R3-1339** `apps/desktop/src/features/pages.browser.spec.tsx:233` — Timestamp filter `!value.includes('T0')` no longer filters timestamps *Fix:* Exclude values that parseTimestamp() accepts instead of matching the substring 'T0'. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the fixture filter drops exactly the four timestamp constants; the old substring test let thirty-six timestamps through as values a page must show.
- **R3-1340** `apps/desktop/src/i18n/i18n.parity.test.ts:32` — The unused-key check counts test files as users of a key *Fix:* Exclude `/\.(test|spec)\.tsx?$/` and `src/test/` from `rest`, as debPackage.wiring.test.ts:115 already does for SCRIPTS. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the dead-key check ignores test files and the test helpers; it had counted a test's mention as a use.

- **R3-1351** `apps/desktop/src/services/approvalRequest.contract.test.ts:127` — Decision-word test omits 'outcome', so that entry can be deleted with the suite green *Fix:* Export the word list and iterate it in the test. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — the decision words are one exported list and the test walks it; with `outcome` deleted the suite had stayed green.

### design-contradiction (130)

- **R3-0500** `CLAUDE.md:116` — Canon says the 2026-08-06 audit left 45 surviving findings; the consolidated index for the same head and date records 122 *Fix:* Say 45 is the filed report's count and that the index holds 77 more from two unfiled rounds.

- **R3-0504** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-independent-audit.md:772` — F-19 gives two line numbers for the same guard in _check_docs_reference_state *Fix:* Use one line number for the guard in both paragraphs.
- **R3-0505** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-independent-audit.md:1806` — The record gives two different counts and line lists for the setup-python pin in ci.yml *Fix:* Note in one of the two places which count was correct at b91f235.
- **R3-0506** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-independent-audit.md:2015` — A Phase 8 item is scored by a 'stub' rule that the record's own rubric does not contain *Fix:* State in the rubric line how a stub is scored, or reclassify the item under the level the rubric does define.
- **R3-0507** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-independent-audit.md:2185` — Challenger note cites the two gate lines one line off from the Part 1 findings *Fix:* Align the line numbers at :2185 to :46 and :42.
- **R3-0508** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-remediation-audit.md:96` — F-01 verdict says it 'held on five of seven' while listing seven attacks of which only attack (5) failed *Fix:* Correct to 'held on six of seven' in an erratum.
- **R3-0509** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-remediation-audit.md:602` — R-05 counts the `reply_binding` grep hits as four; its own review and R-03 count five *Fix:* Correct the walkthrough to five hits.

- **R3-0512** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-remediation-audit.md:2797` — §7 coverage gives different line counts for the same snapshot file in different groups (eight files, each off by one) *Fix:* Note that the per-group counts are approximate, or recount once against the 219c763 snapshot.
- **R3-0513** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-15-zero-trust-reaudit-0a9a1af.md:208` — The fourth report says five forged chains were attacked; it lists six and its results table says six *Fix:* Record the count as six where the archive cites this attack.
- **R3-0514** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-17-sixth-audit-b16e572.md:223` — A-06 says the directory holds reports for the first, third and fourth rounds, omitting the second, which was filed on 2026-08-06 *Fix:* Note in the filing box that the second round's report (remediation-audit) was also present.
- **R3-0515** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-09-19-tenth-audit-75fca65.md:36` — The tenth report gives I-07 as P2; the ninth report that filed it gives P3 *Fix:* Restore P3 in the archive row, or record who raised the severity and why.
- **R3-0516** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/AUDIT_LEDGER.md:81` — A heading says 'Promotions decided by the NINTH independent audit' over a paragraph about the ninth round's findings as decided by the TENTH *Fix:* Rename the heading to 'The ninth round's findings, as decided by the tenth'. **CLOSED before this pass, read in the code 2026-10-13** — the heading is not in the ledger; in the archive it sits over the promotions table and the `I-` findings have their own.

- **R3-0526** `apps/desktop/docs/architecture/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md:51` — Standard transition is specified as 160–240ms; the stylesheet's main motion token is 130ms *Fix:* Either widen the standard to cover 130ms or move the aios tokens onto the menq motion tokens.

- **R3-0530** `apps/desktop/docs/product/AGENT_FLOWS.md:15` — Ten 'canonical' agent statuses incl. 'Waiting approval'; shipped AgentStatus has nine and no such value *Fix:* Either add waiting_approval to AgentStatus (and DATA_MODEL) or state in the flow docs that the approval hold is a run status (awaiting_approval), not…
- **R3-0531** `apps/desktop/docs/product/AGENT_FLOWS.md:36` — Profile claims to render AI_RUNTIME's required agent profile 'plus three fields' but omits Output contract and swaps Approval requirements for Permissions *Fix:* Add Output contract to §2 and correct the count of extra fields to four.
- **R3-0532** `apps/desktop/docs/product/AGENT_FLOWS.md:89` — Lifecycle mapping gives the terminal 'cancelled' no visible status *Fix:* State what the agent chip shows after a cancelled run (e.g. Idle) instead of leaving three terminals mapped to two statuses.
- **R3-0533** `apps/desktop/docs/product/AGENT_FLOWS.md:98` — Pause/resume introduces a paused run condition that is in neither the execution model nor the core statuses the document says it reuses verbatim *Fix:* Say which status a paused run shows (the code's RunStatus has 'paused'; the agent execution model does not).
- **R3-0534** `apps/desktop/docs/product/CHAT_FLOWS.md:95` — A mentioned Offline agent both starts working and has its mention queued *Fix:* Drop 'Offline/' from §2.3 step 4.
- **R3-0535** `apps/desktop/docs/product/CHAT_FLOWS.md:114` — Threads open in a right-side panel here but live in the center pane in this document's own layout and in GROUP_CHAT *Fix:* Say where a thread renders once, and what happens to the right context pane while it is open.

- **R3-0537** `apps/desktop/docs/product/DECISION_APPROVAL_FLOWS.md:18` — Decision lifecycle vocabulary (under review / approved / deferred) is not the one the code knows (open / review / accepted / blocked) *Fix:* Add a mapping row (accepted↔approved, review↔under review) and say deferred has no stored status, or align the code vocabulary.
- **R3-0538** `apps/desktop/docs/product/DECISION_APPROVAL_FLOWS.md:74` — 'MUST NOT invent new states' yet an approved decision 'drops to under review' — a transition the quoted lifecycle does not have *Fix:* Either add the back-transition to AI_RUNTIME's decision states or keep the decision approved and mark propagation blocked, as §1.4 already does.
- **R3-0539** `apps/desktop/docs/product/DECISION_APPROVAL_FLOWS.md:90` — Queue items show 'the approval level (A0–A3)' though A0 never enters the queue and A1 auto-executes *Fix:* Write A2–A3 for queue items.

- **R3-0620** `apps/desktop/src/features/Notifications.strings.ts:165` — The gate title was changed so that no state is called 'sealed', but the chain node for the same blocked read still says 'ENGINE · sealed' *Fix:* Rename the node label to 'ENGINE · refused' to match the title vocabulary. **CLOSED 2026-10-14** — REPRODUCED: a refused governance read was labelled `ENGINE · sealed`. THIS CHANGES WHAT IS SAID: `ENGINE · refused` / `ՇԱՐԺԻՉ · մերժեց` / `ДВИЖОК · отклонил`. The Armenian and Russian word forms are the Builder's.

THIS CHANGES WHAT IS SAID: `Reading…` / `Կարդում ենք…` / `Чтение…`, the first words of the line already under it.

### security (33)

- **R3-0855** `apps/desktop/AUDIT/2026-08-06-remediation-audit.md:130` — The record commits the auditing host's account SID, machine\account name and session scratchpad path *Fix:* Redact the SID, host name and session path to placeholders; they add nothing the finding needs. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — measured wider than the finding: the same account SID is in THREE tracked files — this record, `docs/archive/whole_repo_read_3_2026-10-02.json` (the finding quoting it) and `provision/tests/audit_signer.rs`, where it is the fixture's app SID — and the repository is public, so every past commit carries it whatever the working tree says. Redacting the tree is small; removing it from history is a rewrite of a public repository, and that is the Owner's. Recommended: redact the three files (a made-up SID in the fixture) and leave history alone — it is a local account identifier, not a secret.
- **R3-0856** `apps/desktop/SECURITY.md:60` — The environment-variable table omits BROPS_SELFTEST_MODEL_CMD, which makes the Windows app run `cmd /C <value>` from two commands *Fix:* Add the variable and the subprocess it spawns to SECURITY.md's table and its AI-subprocess section. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the variable is in the table, with what it runs. One detail of the finding is out of date and is not repeated: it is ONE command today — the self-test stopped running a process on 2026-10-09 (`K-05`).
- **R3-0857** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/broker/src/main.rs:181` — With no uid argument the broker admits its own uid as the renderer, the collapse the next comment calls wrong *Fix:* Require the allowed-renderer uid argument and refuse to start when it is absent or equals the broker's own uid. **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (an absent uid), a renderer equal to the broker's uid still OPEN** — REPRODUCED: started with a socket path and nothing else, the broker served; it refuses to start now. The other half was written, pushed and taken back the same day: the live ladder's product-binary phase starts the broker with `"$BROKER_UID"` as the renderer and sends the frame as the broker, so serving that run fail-closed turned its three proofs red. Closing it needs a renderer principal in `engine/ci/live/run_ladder_turn.sh`, which needs root to run and so cannot be run on this box.
- **R3-0858** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/broker/src/main.rs:251` — The F-31 inbound deadline is per read, so an allowed-uid peer dribbling bytes still holds the serial accept loop *Fix:* Give read_one_frame a whole-exchange budget re-armed to the remaining time before each read, as UnixHopConn::read_exact_within_budget does. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `L-24`: `read_one_frame` holds one deadline for the whole frame and re-arms each read to what is left of it.
- **R3-0859** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/broker/src/tcb_probe.rs:38` — The pin manifest's custody check looks at the file only, not at its ancestor directories *Fix:* Run the same ancestor walk over the manifest path that verify_pinned_role runs over each artifact. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `L-29`: the manifest is opened through its judged ancestors, one `openat` per component from `/`.
- **R3-0860** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/agent_bundle.rs:178` — The 'written by the runtime, never from a prompt' rule is a denylist on a self-declared label; verify() never requires written_by == GRANT_WRITER *Fix:* In verify() refuse any grant whose written_by is not exactly GRANT_WRITER (the label can lower trust, never raise it). **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a grant calling its writer `some-other-component` loaded. `verify` refuses every value but `GRANT_WRITER`, exactly; the denylist predicate had no other caller and is removed with its test, whose four names moved into the new one.
- **R3-0861** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/approval_request.rs:176` — Decision-word refusal scans top-level keys only *Fix:* Walk the reply recursively (or refuse any key outside the known reply field set). **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: `{"detail": {"approved": true}}` was read as a clean record. The check walks every key at every depth, through arrays too; values are not scanned.
- **R3-0862** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_sidecar.rs:432` — The 'prefix must actually CHANGE principal' check only tests that the account name appears as a token *Fix:* Say plainly that the check is a spelling check and the supervisor's uid gate is the control, or require the token to follow `-u`. **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (said plainly), the grammar unchanged** — the comment calls it a spelling check and names the supervisor's uid gate as the control. Requiring the token to follow `-u` is `L-36`, which waits on the Owner.
- **R3-0863** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/manifest_authority.rs:95` — Key validity window, revocation and protocol are evaluated once at construction; resolve() has no clock *Fix:* Hold the verified manifest and re-run resolve_production_key with a clock on every resolve, or delete the type. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `L-40`: the authority holds the key's window and reads a clock on every `resolve`. Revocation and protocol are properties of the manifest it was built from and cannot change without a new one.
- **R3-0864** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/tcb_integrity.rs:383` — A pinned path that is not absolute skips the ancestor walk to '/'; nothing refuses it *Fix:* Refuse any artifact whose path does not start with '/' before measuring it. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — by `L-43`: `verify_artifact` refuses a path that is not absolute and normalised before it measures anything.
- **R3-0865** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/windows_broker.rs:284` — verify_image compares digests with no pin-shape check, the defect tcb_integrity fixed *Fix:* Require a 64-lowercase-hex pin in verify_image before comparing. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: an empty pin matched an image that measured as empty text. `verify_image` requires the pin to be 64 lowercase hex, by the POSIX floor's own rule. The function still has no caller on a launch path, as its documentation says.
- **R3-0866** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/proof/src/bin/governed_recorder.rs:802` — Launcher image is hashed on one descriptor and then exec'd by path; its ancestors are never checked *Fix:* Keep the measured launcher fd open and `fexecve` it (or check the launcher/executor/lease ancestors with the same rule as the policy). **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (the directories), the exec still by path** — every directory on the way to the launcher, the executor and the lease is held to the policy's own custody rule before the first is hashed. `fexecve` on the measured descriptor is not done. The live ladder in CI is where the real kit's directories meet the rule.
- **R3-0867** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:280` — WRITE_ACCESS_BITS is called 'every bit that lets a holder change the object' but omits FILE_DELETE_CHILD and FILE_WRITE_EA on a directory *Fix:* Add FILE_DELETE_CHILD (and FILE_WRITE_EA) to the mask used for the ledger directory, as a superset of pipe_acl's. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a signer holding `FILE_DELETE_CHILD` on the ledger directory was accepted. The ledger check uses `LEDGER_WRITE_ACCESS_BITS`, the pipe's mask plus `FILE_DELETE_CHILD` and `FILE_WRITE_EA`; `WRITE_ACCESS_BITS` itself is unchanged, so the drift test against `pipe_acl` still holds.
- **R3-0868** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:725` — verify_key_custody is a denylist: a read ACE for any group outside the 12 WORLD_SIDS passes *Fix:* Refuse any allow ACE whose SID is not SYSTEM, Administrators or the signer. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a read ACE for a local group on the key was accepted. Any ACE granting anything to a principal other than SYSTEM, Administrators or the signer is refused, by a refusal of its own.
- **R3-0869** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:1953` — dacl_facts drops every ACE that is not type ACCESS_ALLOWED, including allow-callback and allow-object ACEs *Fix:* Skip only the known deny types and return AclNotEnumerable for any other ACE type. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — only the four deny types are skipped; any other type that is not a plain allow ACE makes the ACL `AclNotEnumerable`. The rule is a pure function tested on Linux; its five numbers are held to the Windows binding's constants by a compile-time assertion. The Windows half type-checks (`cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`); it was not run on Windows.
- **R3-0870** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:2244` — key_readback_proof treats every Unmeasurable as 'denied the descriptor', but dacl_facts returns it for any error code *Fix:* Classify the GetNamedSecurityInfoW code as classify_failed_key_open does, and accept only ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED as the property. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the read-back's failure is classified by its code: `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` is `DescriptorDenied`, the only refusal `key_readback_proof` accepts as the property; every other code stays `Unmeasurable` and is a refusal. The classifier is tested on Linux; the Windows call site type-checks and was not run on Windows.
- **R3-0871** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/posix_install.rs:604` — The uid the installer and the verify report rest on is the owner of a file created in $TMPDIR, not the kernel's euid *Fix:* Read the uid from /proc/self (already the test oracle) or pin the probe directory instead of trusting TMPDIR. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — on a host with procfs the effective uid is the second field of `/proc/self/status`'s `Uid:` line and `$TMPDIR` is not consulted; a status that is present and states no uid is a refusal. The probe remains for a POSIX host without procfs. NOT reproduced: showing a wrong owner needs a uid-mapping filesystem, which this account cannot mount; the defect is read from the code.
- **R3-0872** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:807` — Sandbox self-heal accepts a directory it did not create *Fix:* On AlreadyExists verify owner, mode 0700 and the marker (via symlink_metadata) or allocate a fresh sandbox name. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: a directory planted at the cached sandbox's name, open to everyone and with a `settings.json` in it, was adopted. On every reuse the directory must be a real directory owned by this account at a mode that lets nobody else in, holding the marker as a real file; one that fails is not repaired, the turn is refused. On Windows only the directory and the marker are checked, as the module already says of its permissions.
- **R3-0873** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — Root contracts/ (the source of the cross-half schemas) is unprotected while its engine copies are protected *Fix:* Add contracts to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `contracts` is in `BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS` and in the test's required list.
- **R3-0874** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — The root-run .deb postinst is not protected *Fix:* Add apps/desktop/src-tauri/deb to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/deb` is in `BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS` and in the test's required list.
- **R3-0875** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — The test suites and engine CI kits that prove the trust code are writable by a turn *Fix:* Add engine/tests, engine/ci and apps/desktop/src-tauri/tests to the list, or state in the doc that tests are deliberately excluded. **WAITS ON THE OWNER (2026-10-13)** — protecting `engine/tests`, `engine/ci` and the desktop's tests stops agent mode from writing a test at all, and saying they are excluded on purpose is the same decision made the other way. Recommended: protect `engine/ci` (the live kits run as root in CI) and leave the two test trees writable, stated in the list's own comment.
- **R3-0876** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1632` — New files under a skip-named directory inside a protected subtree are neither reported nor seen *Fix:* Skip those names only where a build really writes them, or report a newly created skip-named directory. **CLOSED 2026-10-13 (reported), the contents still not read** — REPRODUCED: a turn that created `engine/runtime/build/backdoor.py` produced no notice. A skip-named directory that was not inside the surface when the turn began is named in the notice; what is in it is still not inspected. A new `__pycache__` or `.pytest_cache` is NOT reported — Python makes them on a first test run — and that silence is where `O-1` lives; it is said in the code.
- **R3-0877** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:667` — The renderer can create the 'system-owned' ask conversation kind, and the hidden lookup adopts the newest one *Fix:* Refuse kind 'ask' in the create_conversation command; only hidden_ask_conversation may create it. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — `create_conversation` refuses any kind but `direct` and `group` before it reaches the repository; the frontend asks for no other. Held by the rule's own test and a text check of the handler's order.
- **R3-0878** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:849` — post_user_message lets the renderer choose the author name, which becomes the audit actor id *Fix:* Fix the user author server-side (as create_decision does with Actor::local_operator) or validate it against a non-agent allowlist. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — REPRODUCED: the audit row for a user message named its actor `You`. A user message is attributed to the local operator whatever author it carries; the author stays on the message. The seed test that carried an exception for a demo message naming a person now asserts there is none.
- **R3-0879** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/files.rs:63` — The home-directory clamp compares against HOME only when both HOME and USERPROFILE are set *Fix:* Check the root against every candidate home (HOME and USERPROFILE), not the first one found. **CLOSED 2026-10-13** — the files root is refused if it is ANY home candidate, `HOME` or `USERPROFILE`; the mutant that compares only the first is the old behaviour and turns the new test red.
- **R3-0880** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-broker/proof/isolation_proof.ps1:40` — Default credentials file (plaintext service-account passwords) lives in the directory the header requires to be world-readable *Fix:* Default the creds path outside the staged directory and refuse to run if the file is readable by non-administrators.
- **R3-0881** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/bin/win_signer.rs:27` — The §2.5 floor measures the paths in the pin manifest, never the config the bin loaded or the image that is running *Fix:* Pass the canonical --config path and std::env::current_exe() to the floor and refuse unless they equal the manifest's kit.config and the component's…
- **R3-0882** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/bin/win_tcb_pin.rs:119` — The pinned executor is <bin-dir>\win_executor.exe, not the config's executor_path that the driver actually spawns *Fix:* Have win_tcb_pin read executor_path from config.json and pin that path (refusing if empty), instead of assuming the bin-dir name.
- **R3-0883** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/provision_custody.rs:509` — resolve_trustee_sid fetches the account type and ignores it, so a group name becomes a seed reader *Fix:* Refuse unless use_ is SidTypeUser (or a well-known service SID).
- **R3-0884** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/servers.rs:1451` — Windows signer never checks attestation_protocol or the attestation key set *Fix:* Require exact_keys(attestation, [attestation_protocol, supervisor_key_id, sig]) and compare the protocol, then fix the fixture.
- **R3-0885** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/tcb.rs:68` — The anti-rollback floor is signed with a private seed published in source *Fix:* Treat the signature as a checksum in name and docs, or replace it with a key the deployment directory writer cannot derive (TPM/monotonic counter).
- **R3-0886** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/tcb_floor.rs:38` — The Windows TCB probe measures by path, so the measured object can be swapped before use *Fix:* Open one handle with FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT and take owner, DACL and digest from that handle.
- **R3-0887** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/win-live/src/tcb_floor.rs:250` — Manifest custody checks the pin-manifest file only, not its ancestor directories *Fix:* Apply the same ancestor walk to the manifest path before loading it.
